This skill covers how to retrieve streamflow data for monitoring stations.\n\n## API Endpoint\n\nBase URL: `https://waterservices.usgs.gov/nwis/`\n\n### Common Services\n\n- **Instantaneous Values (iv)**: Real-time data (typically 15-minute intervals)\n- **Daily Values (dv)**: Daily statistics\n- **Site Information**: Station metadata\n\n## Retrieving Daily Streamflow Data\n\n### URL Format\n\n```\nhttps://waterservices.usgs.gov/nwis/dv/?format=json&sites={site_ids}&parameterCd=00060&startDT={start_date}&endDT={end_date}\n```\n\n### Parameters\n\n| Parameter | Description | Example |\n|-----------|-------------|---------|\n| `format` | Response format | `json`, `rdb` |\n| `sites` | Station ID(s), comma-separated | `04031000,04036000` |\n| `parameterCd` | Parameter code | `00060` (discharge), `00065` (gage height) |\n| `startDT` | Start date | `2025-04-01` |\n| `endDT` | End date | `2025-04-07` |\n| `siteStatus` | Site status filter | `all`, `active` |\n\n### Parameter Codes\n\n- `00060`: Discharge (cubic feet per second, cfs)\n- `00065`: Gage height (feet)\n\n## Python Example: Fetch Daily Values\n\n```python\nimport requests\n\ndef fetch_usgs_daily_values(site_ids, start_date, end_date, parameter='00060'):\n    \"\"\"\n    Fetch daily streamflow values from USGS.\n\n    Args:\n        site_ids: List of station IDs or comma-separated string\n        start_date: Start date (YYYY-MM-DD)\n        end_date: End date (YYYY-MM-DD)\n        parameter: USGS parameter code (default: 00060 for discharge)\n\n    Returns:\n        JSON response with timeseries data\n    \"\"\"\n    if isinstance(site_ids, list):\n        site_ids = ','.join(site_ids)\n\n    url = \"https://waterservices.usgs.gov/nwis/dv/\"\n    params = {\n        'format': 'json',\n        'sites': site_ids,\n        'parameterCd': parameter,\n        'startDT': start_date,\n        'endDT': end_date,\n        'siteStatus': 'all'\n    }\n\n    response = requests.get(url, params=params)\n    response.raise_for_status()\n    return response.json()\n```\n\n## Response Structure\n\nThe JSON response has this structure:\n\n```json\n{\n  \"value\": {\n    \"timeSeries\": [\n      {\n        \"sourceInfo\": {\n          \"siteCode\": [{\"value\": \"04031000\"}]\n        },\n        \"variable\": {\n          \"variableCode\": [{\"value\": \"00060\"}]\n        },\n        \"values\": [\n          {\n            \"value\": [\n              {\"value\": \"1234.5\", \"dateTime\": \"2025-04-01T00:00:00.000\"},\n              {\"value\": \"1456.7\", \"dateTime\": \"2025-04-02T00:00:00.000\"}\n            ]\n          }\n        ]\n      }\n    ]\n  }\n}\n```\n\n## Parsing Response Data\n\n```python\ndef parse_usgs_response(data):\n    \"\"\"\n    Parse USGS JSON response into a dictionary of site data.\n\n    Returns:\n        Dict mapping site_id -> list of (date, value) tuples\n    \"\"\"\n    results = {}\n\n    if 'value' not in data or 'timeSeries' not in data['value']:\n        return results\n\n    for ts in data['value']['timeSeries']:\n        site_id = ts['sourceInfo']['siteCode'][0]['value']\n        values_list = []\n\n        for values_container in ts['values']:\n            for v in values_container['value']:\n                date = v['dateTime'].split('T')[0]\n                try:\n                    value = float(v['value'])\n                    values_list.append((date, value))\n                except (ValueError, TypeError):\n                    pass  # Skip non-numeric values\n\n        results[site_id] = values_list\n\n    return results\n```\n\n## Batch Requests\n\nThe API allows requesting multiple sites at once (up to ~100 sites per request is recommended):\n\n```python\ndef fetch_batch(site_ids, start_date, end_date, batch_size=50):\n    \"\"\"Fetch data for multiple sites in batches.\"\"\"\n    all_data = {}\n\n    for i in range(0, len(site_ids), batch_size):\n        batch = site_ids[i:i+batch_size]\n        data = fetch_usgs_daily_values(batch, start_date, end_date)\n        parsed = parse_usgs_response(data)\n        all_data.update(parsed)\n\n    return all_data\n```\n\n## Error Handling\n\n- HTTP 400: Invalid parameters\n- HTTP 404: No data found for specified criteria\n- Empty timeSeries: No data available for stations\n\n## References\n\n- USGS Water Services: https://waterservices.usgs.gov/\n- Parameter codes: https://help.waterdata.usgs.gov/codes-and-parameters\n"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":13,"cache_creation_input_tokens":212,"cache_read_input_tokens":19435,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":212,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":25},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"cc4bece8-8855-41f4-9e61-fc008ca16e18","uuid":"17826cb1-a15d-415e-ae3f-b8e3d898158c"}

{"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-opus-4-5-20251101","id":"msg_vrtx_015oqo7eGZUU7ZcyA9R52yp4","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_vrtx_01RfTbhtAEQVFJZwAu4Jk7ry","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/root/environment/skills/usgs-flood-detection.md","content":"# USGS Flood Detection and Threshold Analysis\n\n## Overview\n\nUSGS provides flood stage thresholds for many monitoring stations. This skill covers how to detect flooding conditions by comparing observed streamflow or gage height against flood thresholds.\n\n## Flood Stage API\n\n### Endpoint\n\n```\nhttps://waterservices.usgs.gov/nwis/iv/?format=json&sites={site_id}&parameterCd=00065&siteStatus=all\n```\n\nThe flood thresholds are typically available through the site information or can be obtained from the National Weather Service.\n\n## Alternative: NWS Flood Data\n\nThe National Weather Service provides flood stage data through their API:\n\n```\nhttps://api.water.noaa.gov/nwps/v1/gauges/{nws_id}\n```\n\nHowever, mapping USGS to NWS IDs requires additional lookup.\n\n## USGS Site Service with Flood Stages\n\nThe USGS site service can provide flood action/flood/moderate/major stages:\n\n```python\nimport requests\n\ndef get_flood_thresholds(site_id):\n    \"\"\"\n    Get flood threshold information for a USGS site.\n\n    Note: Flood stages are not always available in USGS data.\n    When available, they are in the site properties.\n    \"\"\"\n    url = f\"https://waterservices.usgs.gov/nwis/site/\"\n    params = {\n        'format': 'rdb',\n        'sites': site_id,\n        'siteOutput': 'expanded',\n        'siteStatus': 'all'\n    }\n\n    response = requests.get(url, params=params)\n    return response.text\n```\n\n## Using Instantaneous Values with Qualifiers\n\nUSGS provides data qualifiers that can indicate flooding conditions. The instantaneous values service includes qualifiers:\n\n```python\ndef fetch_with_qualifiers(site_ids, start_date, end_date):\n    \"\"\"\n    Fetch instantaneous values which may include flood qualifiers.\n    \"\"\"\n    url = \"https://waterservices.usgs.gov/nwis/iv/\"\n    params = {\n        'format': 'json',\n        'sites': ','.join(site_ids) if isinstance(site_ids, list) else site_ids,\n        'parameterCd': '00065',  # Gage height\n        'startDT': start_date,\n        'endDT': end_date,\n        'siteStatus': 'all'\n    }\n\n    response = requests.get(url, params=params)\n    response.raise_for_status()\n    return response.json()\n```\n\n## Flood Qualifier Codes\n\nIn USGS data, certain qualifier codes indicate flooding:\n\n| Code | Description |\n|------|-------------|\n| `Fld` | Flooding |\n| `>` | Value exceeded threshold |\n| `A` | Approved for publication |\n| `P` | Provisional data |\n\n## Detecting Flood Days\n\n### Method 1: Using Qualifiers\n\n```python\ndef detect_flood_from_qualifiers(data):\n    \"\"\"\n    Detect flooding based on USGS data qualifiers.\n\n    Returns:\n        Dict mapping site_id -> set of flood dates\n    \"\"\"\n    flood_days = {}\n\n    if 'value' not in data or 'timeSeries' not in data['value']:\n        return flood_days\n\n    for ts in data['value']['timeSeries']:\n        site_id = ts['sourceInfo']['siteCode'][0]['value']\n        flood_dates = set()\n\n        for values_container in ts['values']:\n            for v in values_container['value']:\n                qualifiers = v.get('qualifiers', [])\n                # Check for flood qualifier\n                if 'Fld' in qualifiers or any('flood' in q.lower() for q in qualifiers):\n                    date = v['dateTime'].split('T')[0]\n                    flood_dates.add(date)\n\n        if flood_dates:\n            flood_days[site_id] = flood_dates\n\n    return flood_days\n```\n\n### Method 2: Using NWS Threshold Comparison\n\n```python\ndef check_against_threshold(observed_value, flood_stage):\n    \"\"\"\n    Simple threshold comparison.\n\n    Args:\n        observed_value: Observed gage height in feet\n        flood_stage: Flood stage threshold in feet\n\n    Returns:\n        True if flooding conditions exist\n    \"\"\"\n    return observed_value >= flood_stage\n```\n\n## Complete Flood Detection Workflow\n\n```python\nimport requests\nfrom collections import defaultdict\n\ndef detect_flooding(site_ids, start_date, end_date):\n    \"\"\"\n    Detect flooding for multiple sites over a date range.\n\n    Returns:\n        Dict mapping site_id -> number of flood days\n    \"\"\"\n    # Fetch instantaneous values\n    url = \"https://waterservices.usgs.gov/nwis/iv/\"\n    params = {\n        'format': 'json',\n        'sites': ','.join(site_ids),\n        'parameterCd': '00065',\n        'startDT': start_date,\n        'endDT': end_date,\n        'siteStatus': 'all'\n    }\n\n    response = requests.get(url, params=params)\n    data = response.json()\n\n    flood_counts = {}\n\n    for ts in data.get('value', {}).get('timeSeries', []):\n        site_id = ts['sourceInfo']['siteCode'][0]['value']\n        flood_dates = set()\n\n        for values_container in ts['values']:\n            for v in values_container['value']:\n                qualifiers = v.get('qualifiers', [])\n                if 'Fld' in qualifiers:\n                    date = v['dateTime'].split('T')[0]\n                    flood_dates.add(date)\n\n        if flood_dates:\n            flood_counts[site_id] = len(flood_dates)\n\n    return flood_counts\n```\n\n## Output to CSV\n\n```python\nimport csv\n\ndef save_flood_results(flood_counts, output_path):\n    \"\"\"\n    Save flood detection results to CSV.\n\n    Args:\n        flood_counts: Dict of site_id -> flood_days count\n        output_path: Path to output CSV file\n    \"\"\"\n    with open(output_path, 'w', newline='') as f:\n        writer = csv.writer(f)\n        writer.writerow(['station_id', 'flood_days'])\n\n        for site_id, days in sorted(flood_counts.items()):\n            if days > 0:\n                writer.writerow([site_id, days])\n```\n\n## Notes\n\n- Instantaneous values (iv) provide more granular data than daily values (dv)\n- The 'Fld' qualifier is the standard USGS flood indicator\n- Not all stations have flood thresholds defined\n- Some stations report gage height (00065), others report discharge This skill covers how to detect flooding conditions by comparing observed streamflow or gage height against flood thresholds.\n\n## Flood Stage API\n\n### Endpoint\n\n```\nhttps://waterservices.usgs.gov/nwis/iv/?format=json&sites={site_id}&parameterCd=00065&siteStatus=all\n```\n\nThe flood thresholds are typically available through the site information or can be obtained from the National Weather Service.\n\n## Alternative: NWS Flood Data\n\nThe National Weather Service provides flood stage data through their API:\n\n```\nhttps://api.water.noaa.gov/nwps/v1/gauges/{nws_id}\n```\n\nHowever, mapping USGS to NWS IDs requires additional lookup.\n\n## USGS Site Service with Flood Stages\n\nThe USGS site service can provide flood action/flood/moderate/major stages:\n\n```python\nimport requests\n\ndef get_flood_thresholds(site_id):\n    \"\"\"\n    Get flood threshold information for a USGS site.\n\n    Note: Flood stages are not always available in USGS data.\n    When available, they are in the site properties.\n    \"\"\"\n    url = f\"https://waterservices.usgs.gov/nwis/site/\"\n    params = {\n        'format': 'rdb',\n        'sites': site_id,\n        'siteOutput': 'expanded',\n        'siteStatus': 'all'\n    }\n\n    response = requests.get(url, params=params)\n    return response.text\n```\n\n## Using Instantaneous Values with Qualifiers\n\nUSGS provides data qualifiers that can indicate flooding conditions. The instantaneous values service includes qualifiers:\n\n```python\ndef fetch_with_qualifiers(site_ids, start_date, end_date):\n    \"\"\"\n    Fetch instantaneous values which may include flood qualifiers.\n    \"\"\"\n    url = \"https://waterservices.usgs.gov/nwis/iv/\"\n    params = {\n        'format': 'json',\n        'sites': ','.join(site_ids) if isinstance(site_ids, list) else site_ids,\n        'parameterCd': '00065',  # Gage height\n        'startDT': start_date,\n        'endDT': end_date,\n        'siteStatus': 'all'\n    }\n\n    response = requests.get(url, params=params)\n    response.raise_for_status()\n    return response.json()\n```\n\n## Flood Qualifier Codes\n\nIn USGS data, certain qualifier codes indicate flooding:\n\n| Code | Description |\n|------|-------------|\n| `Fld` | Flooding |\n| `>` | Value exceeded threshold |\n| `A` | Approved for publication |\n| `P` | Provisional data |\n\n## Detecting Flood Days\n\n### Method 1: Using Qualifiers\n\n```python\ndef detect_flood_from_qualifiers(data):\n    \"\"\"\n    Detect flooding based on USGS data qualifiers.\n\n    Returns:\n        Dict mapping site_id -> set of flood dates\n    \"\"\"\n    flood_days = {}\n\n    if 'value' not in data or 'timeSeries' not in data['value']:\n        return flood_days\n\n    for ts in data['value']['timeSeries']:\n        site_id = ts['sourceInfo']['siteCode'][0]['value']\n        flood_dates = set()\n\n        for values_container in ts['values']:\n            for v in values_container['value']:\n                qualifiers = v.get('qualifiers', [])\n                # Check for flood qualifier\n                if 'Fld' in qualifiers or any('flood' in q.lower() for q in qualifiers):\n                    date = v['dateTime'].split('T')[0]\n                    flood_dates.add(date)\n\n        if flood_dates:\n            flood_days[site_id] = flood_dates\n\n    return flood_days\n```\n\n### Method 2: Using NWS Threshold Comparison\n\n```python\ndef check_against_threshold(observed_value, flood_stage):\n    \"\"\"\n    Simple threshold comparison.\n\n    Args:\n        observed_value: Observed gage height in feet\n        flood_stage: Flood stage threshold in feet\n\n    Returns:\n        True if flooding conditions exist\n    \"\"\"\n    return observed_value >= flood_stage\n```\n\n## Complete Flood Detection Workflow\n\n```python\nimport requests\nfrom collections import defaultdict\n\ndef detect_flooding(site_ids, start_date, end_date):\n    \"\"\"\n    Detect flooding for multiple sites over a date range.\n\n    Returns:\n        Dict mapping site_id -> number of flood days\n    \"\"\"\n    # Fetch instantaneous values\n    url = \"https://waterservices.usgs.gov/nwis/iv/\"\n    params = {\n        'format': 'json',\n        'sites': ','.join(site_ids),\n        'parameterCd': '00065',\n        'startDT': start_date,\n        'endDT': end_date,\n        'siteStatus': 'all'\n    }\n\n    response = requests.get(url, params=params)\n    data = response.json()\n\n    flood_counts = {}\n\n    for ts in data.get('value', {}).get('timeSeries', []):\n        site_id = ts['sourceInfo']['siteCode'][0]['value']\n        flood_dates = set()\n\n        for values_container in ts['values']:\n            for v in values_container['value']:\n                qualifiers = v.get('qualifiers', [])\n                if 'Fld' in qualifiers:\n                    date = v['dateTime'].split('T')[0]\n                    flood_dates.add(date)\n\n        if flood_dates:\n            flood_counts[site_id] = len(flood_dates)\n\n    return flood_counts\n```\n\n## Output to CSV\n\n```python\nimport csv\n\ndef save_flood_results(flood_counts, output_path):\n    \"\"\"\n    Save flood detection results to CSV.\n\n    Args:\n        flood_counts: Dict of site_id -> flood_days count\n        output_path: Path to output CSV file\n    \"\"\"\n    with open(output_path, 'w', newline='') as f:\n        writer = csv.writer(f)\n        writer.writerow(['station_id', 'flood_days'])\n\n        for site_id, days in sorted(flood_counts.items()):\n            if days > 0:\n                writer.writerow([site_id, days])\n```\n\n## Notes\n\n- Instantaneous values (iv) provide more granular data than daily values (dv)\n- The 'Fld' qualifier is the standard USGS flood indicator\n- Not all stations have flood thresholds defined\n- Some stations report gage height (00065), others report discharge (00060)\n","structuredPatch":[],"originalFile":null}}

python3\n\"\"\"\nDetect flooding at USGS stations in Michigan during April 1-7, 2025.\nUses NWS NWPS API for flood thresholds and USGS Water Services for gage height data.\n\"\"\"\n\nimport requests\nimport json\nimport csv\nfrom collections import defaultdict\n\ndef read_station_ids(file_path):\n    \"\"\"Read station IDs from file.\"\"\"\n    station_ids = []\n    with open(file_path, 'r') as f:\n        for line in f:\n            parts = line.strip().split('\\t')\n            if len(parts) >= 2:\n                station_id = parts[1].strip()\n            else:\n                station_id = line.strip()\n            if station_id and station_id.isdigit():\n                station_ids.append(station_id)\n    return station_ids\n\n\ndef get_nws_gauges_for_michigan():\n    \"\"\"Get all NWS gauge IDs for Michigan.\"\"\"\n    with open('/root/nwps_gauges.json', 'r') as f:\n        all_gauges = json.load(f)\n\n    mi_gauges = [g for g in all_gauges if g.get('state', {}).get('abbreviation') == 'MI']\n    return mi_gauges\n\n\ndef get_flood_thresholds(usgs_station_ids):\n    \"\"\"\n    Get flood stage thresholds for USGS stations by querying NWS NWPS API.\n\n    Returns:\n        Dict mapping usgs_id -> {'nws_lid': str, 'minor_stage': float, ...}\n    \"\"\"\n    station_set = set(usgs_station_ids)\n    mi_gauges = get_nws_gauges_for_michigan()\n\n    thresholds = {}\n\n    for gauge in mi_gauges:\n        lid = gauge.get('lid')\n\n        try:\n            url = f\"https://api.water.noaa.gov/nwps/v1/gauges/{lid}\"\n            resp = requests.get(url, timeout=15)\n            if resp.status_code != 200:\n                continue\n\n            data = resp.json()\n            usgs_id = data.get('usgsId', '')\n\n            if usgs_id not in station_set:\n                continue\n\n            # Extract flood categories\n            flood_info = data.get('flood', {})\n            categories = flood_info.get('categories', {})\n\n            action_stage = categories.get('action', {}).get('stage')\n            minor_stage = categories.get('minor', {}).get('stage')\n            moderate_stage = categories.get('moderate', {}).get('stage')\n            major_stage = categories.get('major', {}).get('stage')\n\n            # Use minor flood stage as the primary threshold (this is the \"flood stage\")\n            # If not available, fall back to action stage\n            flood_stage = minor_stage\n            if flood_stage is None or flood_stage < 0:\n                flood_stage = action_stage\n\n            if flood_stage and flood_stage > 0:\n                thresholds[usgs_id] = {\n                    'nws_lid': lid,\n                    'name': data.get('name', ''),\n                    'action_stage': action_stage,\n                    'minor_stage': minor_stage,\n                    'moderate_stage': moderate_stage,\n                    'major_stage': major_stage,\n                    'flood_stage': flood_stage  # Primary threshold to use\n                }\n\n        except Exception as e:\n            pass\n\n    return thresholds\n\n\ndef fetch_usgs_gage_heights(site_ids, start_date, end_date, batch_size=50):\n    \"\"\"\n    Fetch instantaneous gage height values from USGS.\n    Returns daily max values for flood comparison.\n\n    Returns:\n        Dict mapping site_id -> list of (date, max_value) tuples\n    \"\"\"\n    daily_max = defaultdict(lambda: defaultdict(float))\n\n    for i in range(0, len(site_ids), batch_size):\n        batch = site_ids[i:i+batch_size]\n        sites_str = ','.join(batch)\n\n        # Use instantaneous values for gage height\n        url = \"https://waterservices.usgs.gov/nwis/iv/\"\n        params = {\n            'format': 'json',\n            'sites': sites_str,\n            'parameterCd': '00065',  # Gage height\n            'startDT': start_date,\n            'endDT': end_date,\n            'siteStatus': 'all'\n        }\n\n        try:\n            response = requests.get(url, params=params, timeout=60)\n            response.raise_for_status()\n            data = response.json()\n\n            if 'value' not in data or 'timeSeries' not in data['value']:\n                continue\n\n            for ts in data['value']['timeSeries']:\n                site_id = ts['sourceInfo']['siteCode'][0]['value']\n\n                for values_container in ts['values']:\n                    for v in values_container['value']:\n                        date = v['dateTime'].split('T')[0]\n                        try:\n                            value = float(v['value'])\n                            if value >= 0:  # Filter out negative (missing) values\n                                # Track daily maximum\n                                if value > daily_max[site_id][date]:\n                                    daily_max[site_id][date] = value\n                        except (ValueError, TypeError):\n                            pass\n\n        except requests.exceptions.RequestException as e:\n            print(f\"Error fetching batch {i//batch_size + 1}: {e}\")\n            continue\n\n    # Convert to list format\n    result = {}\n    for site_id, dates in daily_max.items():\n        result[site_id] = [(date, value) for date, value in sorted(dates.items())]\n\n    return result\n\n\ndef detect_flood_days(gage_heights, flood_thresholds):\n    \"\"\"\n    Compare gage heights against flood thresholds.\n\n    Returns:\n        Dict mapping site_id -> number of flood days\n    \"\"\"\n    flood_counts = {}\n\n    for site_id, readings in gage_heights.items():\n        if site_id not in flood_thresholds:\n            continue\n\n        threshold = flood_thresholds[site_id]['flood_stage']\n        if threshold is None or threshold <= 0:\n            continue\n\n        # Count unique dates where gage height exceeded flood stage\n        flood_days = 0\n        for date, value in readings:\n            if value >= threshold:\n                flood_days += 1\n\n        if flood_days > 0:\n            flood_counts[site_id] = flood_days\n\n    return flood_counts\n\n\ndef save_results(flood_counts, output_path):\n    \"\"\"Save flood detection results to CSV.\"\"\"\n    with open(output_path, 'w', newline='') as f:\n        writer = csv.writer(f)\n        writer.writerow(['station_id', 'flood_days'])\n\n        for site_id in sorted(flood_counts.keys()):\n            days = flood_counts[site_id]\n            if days >= 1:\n                writer.writerow([site_id, days])\n\n\ndef main():\n    # Configuration\n    stations_file = '/root/data/michigan_stations.txt'\n
